Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself feeling more down on yourself lately, like you’re not worth much or that you’re letting people down, even if you’re doing your best?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I suppose I do, more than I’d like to admit.\n\nI try not to sit around feeling sorry for myself, but I keep thinking I kind of failed everybody when I lost my old place… like I should’ve kept things together better than I did. If the church pantry is short on supplies, or if I miss a hallway at work, I start thinking, “Well, that’s on me,” even when maybe it isn’t all mine to carry.\n\nI know that probably doesn’t sound very logical… but that’s where my mind goes. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you felt unusually high, overly energetic, or so good that you didn’t feel like you needed much sleep, even if just for a few days?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not really like that.\n\nI mean, I’ve had days where I’m in a better mood or I get a little more done than usual, but not where I felt, you know, on top of the world or like I didn’t need sleep for days. for the front door.\n- key: S020\n  name: Hallucinations\n  description: Perception-like experiences that occur without an external stimulus and are as vivid and clear as normal perception.\n  manifestation: When I’m mopping after hours, I’ll hear a man’s voice say my name or tell me I’ve missed a spot, clear as if he’s standing by the stairwell, but when I look there’s nobody there.\n- key: S021\n  name: Disorganized_Thinking_Speech\n  description: Symptoms where speech or thought appears scattered and difficult to follow due to a lack of logical or consistent flow.\n  manifestation: Sometimes when my supervisor asks me a simple question about which floors I finished, I start talking about the old bus route, the garden plot, and the church basement, and I can hear myself not getting back to the answer.\n- key: S022\n  name: Disorganized_Abnormal_Behavior\n  description: A state where behavior and motor control are severely disturbed, appearing as movements or reactions inappropriate to the situation.\n  manifestation: There have been times at work when I keep wiping the same patch of glass over and over or stand in the supply closet holding the broom because it feels like I have to wait for the right sign before I can move on.\n- key: S023\n  name: Negative_Symptoms\n  description: A state where emotions, motivation, speech, or social interest that should normally be present are diminished or lost.\n  manifestation: What brought me in is I don’t feel like myself anymore—I barely talk to people, I just go flat and quiet, and even getting started on the cleaning route feels like pushing a loaded cart uphill.\n- key: S025\n  name: Loss_of_Interest\n  description: A state where interest or pleasure in previously enjoyed activities is markedly diminished.\n  manifestation: I used to look forward to fishing on Saturdays, doing crosswords with coffee, or showing the younger guys tricks for stripping a floor, but now I mostly let those things sit because they don’t give me anything back.\n- key: S026\n  name: Weight_Appetite_Change\n  description: Significant increase or decrease in appetite or weight.\n  manifestation: Most days food just tastes like cardboard, so I skip meals or eat a few crackers after my shift, and my work pants have been hanging looser than they used to.\n- key: S029\n  name: Fatigue\n  description: Persistent fatigue that is not relieved by rest and a marked lack of energy.\n  manifestation: Even if I sleep through the night, my arms and legs feel heavy by the time I get to the building, and small jobs like hauling trash bags wear me out more than they should.\n- key: S030\n  name: Worthlessness_Guilt\n  description: Feelings of worthlessness or excessive or inappropriate guilt (which may be delusional; not merely self-reproach or guilt about being sick).\n  manifestation: I keep thinking I failed everybody by losing my old place and not keeping my life together, like if the church pantry is short on supplies or a hallway gets missed, somehow that’s on me too.\n- key: S032\n  name: Death_Suicide\n  description: Recurrent thoughts of death, recurrent suicidal ideation, or a suicide attempt.\n  manifestation: I don’t have some big plan, but a lot of mornings I wake up thinking it might be easier if I just didn’t wake up at all, and that scares me enough to say it out loud.\n- key: additional_requirement_1\n  name: Additional Requirement 1\n  description: psychotic_symptoms and depressive_episode occur concurrently\n  manifestation: When I was at my lowest—barely eating, feeling guilty, and thinking I might not want to wake up—I was also hearing that man’s voice at work and believing people in the building were watching me.\n- key: additional_requirement_2\n  name: Additional Requirement 2\n  description: depressive_episode is present for the majority of the total duration of the active and residual portions of the illness (Criterion C)\n  manifestation: For most of the time since the move, the sadness, low energy, guilt, and loss of interest have been there in the background, even on days when the voices or camera worries were the main thing on my mind.\n- key: additional_requirement_3\n  name: Additional Requirement 3\n  description: delusions_and_hallucinations occur for 2 or more weeks in the absence of a major mood episode (Criterion B)\n  manifestation: There was a stretch of several weeks when my mood had lifted some and I was getting through meals and work a little better, but I still heard my name in the stairwell and stayed convinced the hallway cameras were being used to track me.\n- key: functional_impairment\n  name: Functional Impairment\n  description: required\n  manifestation: My supervisor started writing up missed routes and repeated cleaning mistakes, and I stopped keeping up with church, fishing, and the people I used to check in on. I finally went to the employee assistance program because I could not keep my work routine straight anymore.\n- key: duration\n  name: Duration\n  description: 1 year and 5 months\n  manifestation: This has been building for about 1 year and 5 months, ever since I had to leave my old apartment and neighborhood.\n- key: psychotic_symptoms_duration\n  name: Psychotic Symptoms Duration\n  description: 11 months\n  manifestation: The voices and worries about being watched have been going on for about 11 months now.\n- key: depressive_episode_duration\n  name: Depressive Episode Duration\n  description: 1 year and 2 months\n  manifestation: The depression part has been with me for about 1 year and 2 months, starting not long after the move.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 59 / janitor_or_building_cleaner / some_college\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S023\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Have you found yourself feeling more down on yourself lately, like you’re not worth much or that you’re letting people down, even if you’re doing your best?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I suppose I do, more than I’d like to admit.\n\nI try not to sit around feeling sorry for myself, but I keep thinking I kind of failed everybody when I lost my old place… like I should’ve kept things together better than I did. And then it spills over into other things too. If the church pantry is short on supplies, or if I miss a hallway at work, I start thinking, “Well, that’s on me,” even when maybe it isn’t all mine to carry.\n\nI know that probably doesn’t sound very logical… but that’s where my mind goes. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you felt unusually high, overly energetic, or so good that you didn’t feel like you needed much sleep, even if just for a few days?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not really like that.\n\nI mean, I’ve had days where I’m in a better mood or I get a little more done than usual, but not where I felt, you know, on top of the world or like I didn’t need sleep for days. If anything, I usually still want the sleep.\n\nIs that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving more slowly than usual, or maybe feeling restless and unable to sit still, like your body is matching how your mind is feeling?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’ve noticed that, exactly.\n\nI mean, I move slower sometimes when I’m tired, I guess, but I don’t know if it’s more than anybody else would. pantry is short on supplies or a hallway gets missed, somehow that’s on me too.\n- key: S032\n  name: Death_Suicide\n  description: Recurrent thoughts of death, recurrent suicidal ideation, or a suicide attempt.\n  manifestation: I don’t have some big plan, but a lot of mornings I wake up thinking it might be easier if I just didn’t wake up at all, and that scares me enough to say it out eating, feeling guilty, and thinking I might not want to wake up—I was also hearing that man’s voice at work and believing people in the building were watching me.\n- key: additional_requirement_2\n manifestation: Sometimes when my supervisor asks me a simple question about which floors I finished, I start talking about the old bus route, the garden plot, and the church basement, and I can hear those things sit because they don’t give me anything back.\n- key: S026\n  name: Weight_Appetite_Change\n  description: Significant increase or decrease in appetite or weight.\n  manifestation: Most days food just tastes like cardboard, so I skip meals or eat a few crackers after my shift, and my work pants have been hanging looser than they used to.\n- key: S029\n  name: Fatigue\n  description: Persistent fatigue that is not relieved by rest and a marked lack of energy.\n  manifestation: Even if I sleep through the night, my arms and legs feel heavy by the time I get to the building, and small jobs like hauling trash bags wear me out more than they should.\n- key: S030\n  name: Worthlessness_Guilt\n  description: Feelings of worthlessness or excessive or inappropriate guilt (which may be delusional; not merely self-reproach or guilt about being sick).\n  manifestation: I keep thinking I failed everybody by losing my old place and not keeping my life together, like if the church pantry is short on supplies or a hallway gets missed, somehow that’s on me too.\n- key: S032\n  name: Death_Suicide\n  description: Recurrent thoughts of death, recurrent suicidal ideation, or a suicide attempt.\n  manifestation: I don’t have some big plan, but a lot of mornings I wake up thinking it might be easier if I just didn’t wake up at all, and that scares me enough to say it out Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself feeling more down on yourself lately, like you’re not worth much or that you’re letting people down, even if you’re doing your best?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I suppose I do, more than I’d like to admit.\n\nI try not to sit around feeling sorry for myself, but I keep thinking I kind of failed everybody when I lost my old place… like I should’ve kept things together better than I did. And then it spills over into other things too. If the church pantry is short on supplies, or if I miss a hallway at work, I start thinking, “Well, that’s on me,” even when maybe it isn’t all mine to carry.\n\nI know that probably doesn’t sound very logical… but that’s where my mind goes. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you felt unusually high, overly energetic, or so good that you didn’t feel like you needed much sleep, even if just for a few days?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not really like that.\n\nI mean, I’ve had days where I’m in a better mood or I get a little more done than usual, but not where I felt, you know, on top of the world or like I didn’t need sleep for days. If anything, I usually still want the sleep.\n\nIs that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving more slowly than usual, or maybe feeling restless and unable to sit still, like your body is matching how your mind is feeling?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’ve noticed that, exactly.\n\nI mean, I move slower sometimes when I’m tired, I guess, but I don’t know if it’s more than anybody else would. And I don’t think I’m pacing around or unable to sit still, not in a way I’ve really paid attention to.\n\nMaybe I should watch for it more… but right now I don’t think I could say yes to that. Is that okay?\n\nDoctor: Have you had days when you feel down or lose interest in things almost all day, nearly every day, for a period of time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that does happen. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but there are stretches where I just don’t really feel much pull toward things.\n\nLike, I used to look forward to fishing on Saturdays, or sitting with coffee and doing crosswords. Even at work, I used to kind of enjoy showing the younger guys little tricks for stripping a floor the right way. Lately I mostly just… let those things sit. They don’t give me much back, if that makes sense.\n\nI still try to keep going, but the interest part, it’s been pretty low. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period before this one where you felt this way for several weeks or more, where it seemed like you were just going through the motions most days?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure… I mean, I’ve had rough patches before, sure, but I don’t know if I could honestly say it was several weeks or more in the same way.\n\nSometimes life just feels kind of routine, you know—work, home, sleep, do it again. But whether that was me being “down” like this, or just tired and getting older, I’m not positive.\n\nI don’t want to overstate it. I guess I’d say maybe, but I’d have to think on it a little more. Is that an okay answer?\n\nDoctor: Can you think back and tell me if there was ever a time before this current period when you felt down or lost interest in things nearly every day for at least two weeks in a row?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I’m trying to think back. Even at work, I liked showing the younger guys little tricks for stripping a floor right. Now I mostly just… let those things sit. They don’t really give me much back, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your energy levels or do you feel more tired than usual, even when you haven’t been doing much?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t want to overstate it, but I have been more worn out than I think I should be.\n\nEven if I sleep through the night, by the time I get to the building my arms and legs can feel kind of heavy. And little things, like hauling trash bags, seem to take more out of me than they used to. I still get through it, mostly, but… it’s harder than I’d like to admit. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself feeling more down on yourself lately, like you’re not worth much or that you’re letting people down, even if you’re doing your best?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I suppose I do, more than I’d like to admit.\n\nI try not to sit around feeling sorry for myself, but I keep thinking I kind of failed everybody when I lost my old place… like I should’ve kept things together better than I did. And then it spills over into other things too. If the church pantry is short on supplies, or if I miss a hallway at work, I start thinking, “Well, that’s on me,” even when maybe it isn’t all mine to carry.\n\nI know that probably doesn’t sound very logical… but that’s where my mind goes. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you felt unusually high, overly energetic, or so good that you didn’t feel like you needed much sleep, even if just for a few days?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not really like that.\n\nI mean, I’ve had days where I’m in a better mood or I get a little more done than usual, but not where I felt, you know, on top of the world or like I didn’t need sleep for days. If anything, I usually still want the sleep.\n\nIs that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving more slowly than usual, or maybe feeling restless and unable to sit still, like your body is matching how your mind is feeling?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’ve noticed that, exactly.\n\nI mean, I move slower sometimes when I’m tired, I guess, but I don’t know if it’s more than anybody else would. And I don’t think I’m pacing around or unable to sit still, not in a way I’ve really paid attention to.\n\nMaybe I should watch for it more… but right now I don’t think I could say yes to that. Is that okay?\n\nDoctor: Have you had days when you feel down or lose interest in things almost all day, nearly every day, for a period of time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that does happen. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but there are stretches where I just don’t really feel much pull toward things.\n\nLike, I used to look forward to fishing on Saturdays, or sitting with coffee and doing crosswords. Even at work, I used to kind of enjoy showing the younger guys little tricks for stripping a floor the right way. Lately I mostly just… let those things sit. They don’t give me much back, if that makes sense.\n\nI still try to keep going, but the interest part, it’s been pretty low. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period before this one where you felt this way for several weeks or more, where it seemed like you were just going through the motions most days?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure… I mean, I’ve had rough patches before, sure, but I don’t know if I could honestly say it was several weeks or more in the same way.\n\nSometimes life just feels kind of routine, you know—work, home, sleep, do it again. But whether that was me being “down” like this, or just tired and getting older, I’m not positive.\n\nI don’t want to overstate it. I guess I’d say maybe, but I’d have to think on it a little more. Is that an okay answer?\n\nDoctor: Can you think back and tell me if there was ever a time before this current period when you felt down or lost interest in things nearly every day for at least two weeks in a row?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I’m trying to think back. I don’t know if I can pin down another time before this one where it was clearly two weeks straight, nearly every day.\n\nWhat I can say is this current stretch has that feeling — like I used to look forward to things, fishing on Saturdays, or coffee and a crossword, even showing the younger guys at work how to strip a floor right. And now I mostly just let those things sit because they don’t really give me anything back.\n\nBefore this, maybe I had little spells like that, but I’m not sure I’d want to say it was that long unless I knew. I don’t want to answer wrong. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Can you think back and tell me if there was ever a time before this current period when you felt down or lost interest in things nearly every day for at least two weeks in a row?\n\nPatient: I wish I could give you a cleaner yes or no, but I’m not sure.\n\nBefore this current stretch, I know I had times where I felt worn down or kind of flat, but I don’t remember clearly that it was nearly every day for two full weeks. I don’t want to say yes just to be agreeable if I can’t really back it up.\n\nSo… maybe there were shorter patches, but I can’t honestly point to one before this and say, “yes, that was at least two weeks.” Is that alright?\n\nDoctor: Can you remember having more than one time in your life when you felt down or lost interest in things nearly every day for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I can say more than one, no… not clearly.\n\nI mean, I’ve had times in life where I wasn’t exactly cheerful, but when you say nearly every day for two weeks, that’s pretty specific. I can’t honestly remember multiple times like that.\n\nSo I’d say… not that I can remember. I hope that’s a useful answer.\n\nDoctor: Looking back, can you recall if there was ever a time before this current period when you felt down or lost interest in things for at least two weeks straight?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure before this current one, if I’m being honest. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it was.\n\nRight now, though, yeah… it’s like the things I used to look forward to don’t really pull me anymore. Fishing on Saturdays, coffee and a crossword, even showing the younger guys at work little tricks for stripping a floor — I used to get something out of that. Lately I mostly just let those things sit, because they don’t give me much back.\n\nBut before this period… I can’t clearly say there was another full two weeks like that. Is that the kind of thing you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Can you remember a time before this current period when you had a clear improvement in your mood and functioning for at least two months, where you were back to feeling more like your usual self?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I can point to a clear stretch like that, honestly.\n\nI mean, there have been days or maybe little stretches where I functioned better, went to work, kept up more, and I probably seemed more like myself. But “at least two months” where I was clearly improved and back to usual… I don’t want to say yes if I can’t really remember it that cleanly.\n\nSo I guess my answer is, I’m not sure. I don’t have a specific time in mind that fits that. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Looking back, can you recall if there was ever a time before this current period when you had a clear improvement in your mood and functioning for at least two months, where you were back to feeling more like your usual self?\n\nPatient: I know I kind of answered that already, but… no, I don’t think I can really pick out a solid two-month stretch like that before this.\n\nThere might’ve been times I was doing a bit better, keeping up with things more, and I don’t want to make it sound like it was all bad.
